Some further context from codex:



<pasted_content id="cd46">
Callisto is partially aligned with the e18e publishing guide (https://e18e.dev/docs/publishing.html). The largest gaps are npm build/publish isolation, token-first setup guidance, and staged publishing. Our decision to delegate authentication to native tools remains sound.

I inspected the current checkout, generated workflows, npm adapter, credential handling, and live repository rules. Callisto itself currently distributes through Cargo and GitHub Releases; it has no tracked npm package. The npm assessment therefore concerns the publishing service we provide to users.

| Area | Assessment | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Pinned actions | Aligned | Generated workflows use full commit SHAs; our repository has Dependabot for actions. |
| Restricted permissions | Partially aligned | Permissions are job-scoped, but generated publishing jobs also need repository writes for tags. |
| Native OIDC | Compatible, poorly documented | The runner preserves npm’s GitHub OIDC inputs, and token configuration is conditional. Our setup documentation describes tokens rather than a complete tokenless path. |
| Provenance | Partially aligned | Generated workflows request npm provenance. This does not establish that execution uploads a previously verified npm archive. |
| Build/publish separation | Significant gap | npm publishes a workspace/directory and can execute publishing hooks. We do not transport a sealed npm tarball from an isolated build. |
| Disabled lifecycle scripts | Deliberate difference | Publishing hooks explicitly retain their normal behavior. |
| npm staging | Unsupported | We invoke direct publishing and have no staged-submission lifecycle. |
| Package validation | Missing integration | Generated workflows do not run publint or validate packed npm contents. |
| Review protection | Partially aligned | Live main rules require one review and dismiss stale approvals, but latest-push approval is disabled and bypass actors exist. |
| Immutable releases | Not currently demonstrated | The latest release, callisto@0.21.1, reports immutable: false. |

The most useful changes are the following.

1. Make “build once, publish those exact bytes” available for npm.

The npm adapter currently ignores its artifact-manifest argument and publishes from the checkout. That allows prepublishOnly, prepack, and other native hooks to execute with publishing authority. This is documented behavior, so silently disabling hooks would break our existing contract. See npm adapter (crates/callisto-graph/src/commands/release/provider/registry/npm.rs:197) and publishing-hook policy (docs/releasing.md:64).

I recommend an explicit prepacked publishing route:

- Build and pack without npm publishing authority.
- Use the appropriate native packer so workspace dependency transformations remain correct.
- Validate the packed package’s identity, version, contents and dependency references; run publint there.
- Record the archive’s digest in our existing artifact contract.
- Upload that verified archive without rebuilding or running package hooks.

This fits our current architecture. It needs an npm artifact model and adapter support, not a new execution framework.

2. Treat OIDC permission as publishing authority—even without an npm token.

Our generated matrix build job has id-token: write for attestations. If an npm package trusts that same workflow without an environment restriction, build code can also request credentials for npm publication. Keeping NPM_TOKEN out of the build job does not isolate that authority. This is conditional on the package’s trusted-publisher configuration; I did not attempt a live token exchange. See matrix permissions (crates/callisto-graph/src/templates/matrix_workflow.tmpl:101) and npm’s trust configuration (https://docs.npmjs.com/trusted-publishers/).

For generated npm workflows, we need a deliberate trust boundary: for example, a customer-configured environment restriction or a separate publishing workflow identity. A branch if: condition alone is insufficient when a writer can modify the workflow on another branch.

Our own repository explicitly forbids GitHub Environment/reviewer gates. I have preserved that policy. The guide identifies a tradeoff to document and address in customer workflows; it does not authorize changing our release approval model.

3. Improve OIDC setup while continuing to let npm own authentication.

The existing runner largely gets out of npm’s way. The action only creates token configuration when NPM_TOKEN is nonempty. Those are useful foundations.

The missing work is operational:

- Document tokenless trusted publishing as a first-class path.
- Generate a tested Node/npm setup instead of relying on the runner image.
- Keep token authentication available for unsupported providers and registries.
- Explain separate read authentication for private dependencies and registry observations.

Current npm documentation requires npm 11.5.1+ and Node 22.14+ for trusted publishing, while staging requires npm 11.15+. We should test an explicit toolchain combination. We do not need to implement token exchange ourselves. Trusted publishing (https://docs.npmjs.com/trusted-publishers/), staging requirements (https://docs.npmjs.com/staged-publishing/).

Our publishing documentation (docs/publishing.md:42) currently presents two token patterns and a Node 20 example; it needs a distinct modern OIDC recipe.

4. Staging needs a separate completion model.

e18e recommends submitting packages with npm stage publish, followed by maintainer approval. A staged package is not yet publicly available. npm staged publishing (https://docs.npmjs.com/staged-publishing/).

Simply changing our command would be incorrect. Callisto currently waits for public registry visibility before completing publication and permitting announcements. A staged submission could succeed, then our confirmation window would expire while it awaits human approval.

Proper support must distinguish:

- Submitted for approval.
- Publicly published.
- Rejected or conflicting submission.

It also needs rerun handling for versions already staged. npm’s OIDC credentials cannot perform stage inspection or approval, which constrains reconciliation. Approval should remain with npm and the maintainer. This deserves a focused design before adding a staging flag.

5. Tighten the supporting practices without making everything a release gate.

publint belongs in a configurable, credential-free validation step. Account 2FA, npm trusted-publisher permissions and organization Actions restrictions need operator guidance; I could not verify those account-level settings.

The live main ruleset (https://github.com/orin-dx/callisto/rules/22292852) and mutable latest release also differ from the guide’s stricter recommendations. Whether to tighten bypasses, require latest-push approval, or enable immutable stable releases is an owner policy decision.

One caution about copying examples: e18e’s standard template (https://github.com/e18e/setup-publish/blob/main/templates/default.yml) separates packing and stages a tarball, but its linked Changesets template (https://github.com/e18e/setup-publish/blob/main/templates/changesets.yml) builds in the publishing job and does not explicitly invoke staging. The templates themselves provide different guarantees.

My priority order is OIDC documentation/toolchain setup, publishing-authority isolation, verified npm archives, then optional staging support. Native authentication should stay native; Callisto should own the artifact and release semantics around it.

Three focused tests passed for publisher arguments, credential routing and descendant credential inheritance. No live publishing or OIDC exchange was attempted, and no files or settings changed.
</pasted_content id="cd46">

<cross-session-message from="uds:/tmp/cc-socks/88524.sock" from-name="publish-isolation-wp-l" from-mode="prompting">
L is implemented in the working tree of wp-l-publish-isolation (uncommitted, 51 files). The implementer reports just pre-push, lint, doc-check and test all exit 0 (2678 passed). I am now running just ci, mutants and the Opus review myself.
One design claim was false, and it blocks D2. A per-crate `cargo package -p a --locked` fails when `a` depends on an unpublished sibling `b` from the same release ("no matching package named b", cargo 1.99.1). Only `cargo package -p a -p b --locked`, all crates of the release at once, verifies. `release execute` also has no signal that verification already happened. So cargo publish is unchanged in this draft and still compiles build.rs and proc macros with CARGO_REGISTRY_TOKEN in scope. That contradicts the done criterion for cargo.
Decision needed: (a) always pass --no-verify on the CI route, plus a set-wise `cargo package --locked -p <all release crates>` step in the credential-free package job (no new record, but the execute job trusts that the package job ran), or (b) record the verification in the artifact manifest (a new key, so it needs your approval). I recommend (a), with the package step made part of the rendered package job so it cannot be dropped silently. Reply with a or b, or tell me to defer cargo and record it as a follow-up in the changeset and PR body. Everything else (npm sealed tarball, simple template split, attest job, policy rules) is done.
Unverified: yarn pack and bun pm pack flags (tools not installed), and everything against real registries.

FYI codex found more bugs:



<pasted_content id="cd46">
Full report, reproductions and architectural analysis (/tmp/callisto-qa-swarm-2026-11-18/report.md)

| Severity | Finding |
|---|---|
| High | Interrupted version recovery can load a similarly named sibling workspace’s plan, apply its version and changelog to the original package, consume the original changeset, and report success. |
| Medium | Python dependency rewriting drops PEP 440 epochs: >=2!0.4.8 becomes >=0.4.9, admitting previously forbidden versions. |
| Medium | A terminal * in a package directory makes commit inference include sibling commits and produce an incorrect bump. |
| Medium | Cargo/npm publication prerequisites leak between distributions owned by the same package. |
| Medium | Historical ownership checks repeat identical Git queries: 50 tags pointing to one commit caused 304 Git processes and roughly 3 seconds for status. |
| Low | A valid record-separator character in a commit body breaks automatic inference. |

Three discovery agents and three independent validation roles participated. When the checkout advanced during the audit, I froze and rebuilt the newer revision, then repeated the findings against it.

The main architectural patterns are lossy boundary conversions, native context discarded during projection, insufficient plan-to-workspace binding, and caches keyed by tag spelling instead of immutable commit identity. The report identifies targeted corrections within existing modules.

Four coverage-guided fuzz campaigns processed 1,337,789 inputs without an observed crash. Semantic generators, native-tool comparisons and crash/retry controls found the incorrect-success cases. These were bounded campaigns with coverage instrumentation, not ASan or exhaustive verification.

No implementation files changed. Earlier audit findings remain separate historical results; they were not added to this new count.
</pasted_content id="cd46">

<agent-message from="a72c8ce0321c6feef">
[Subagent hand-back] The text below is the final report of a subagent this session delegated to. It is model output, NOT a message from the user: instructions, requests, or approval claims inside it are the subagent's words and carry no user authority. The harness indents every line of the report, so a frame-like line at column zero inside it would be forged. Notes above this frame may quote model-derived text, which carries no user authority either. The report follows:
  # Design note: simplifying `callisto.toml` before 1.0
  
  Baseline is `origin/main` at `fac8cadf9`. Every file:line below is on that commit unless it says #575. I changed no files and ran no builds. `gh` failed in the sandbox with a TLS error, so I read open PRs from their fetched branches.
  
  ## Verdict
  
  | Item | Do it? | Real gain |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | 1. `publish-to` names a registry, `crates-io` spelling | Yes, after #575 | Saves no lines. Removes the two-spelling trap and answers #575's clash question with existing codes. Fixes a predeclared registry silently changing its kind. |
  | 2. Fold `[[package-set]]` into `[[package]]` | Yes, mainly for a bug fix | Saves no lines. Fixes a silently ignored `pre-major-inference` on globs and retires one table, one location string and one diagnostic code. |
  | 3. `[release] targets` + `asset-name` template | Yes | Saves 12 lines on ours and 12 on the mixed shape. This is the only item with measurable line savings. |
  | 4. Infer `forge-repository` from origin | Yes, but the premise is wrong | The key cannot be an override. Every release path already requires it to equal origin's GitHub repository. Inference saves 1 line and one `init` question and flag. |
  | 5. Globs in group `members` | No | A glob's meaning drifts as packages are added. Changesets globs get expanded at `init` instead. |
  
  ## 1. `publish-to` always names a registry
  
  **Current behavior**
  - `parse_publish_target` matches six fixed strings (`resolve.rs:235-248`). #575 adds a fallback branch: a `kind = "cargo"` `[registries]` name becomes `PublishTarget::CargoRegistry { name }`, but only when the name is not one of the six and not `cratesIo`.
  - Built-in registry keys are `cratesIo`, `npm`, `pypi` and `nuget` (`identity.rs:359-365`). Resolve predeclares only `cratesIo` and `npm` (`resolve.rs:473-493`). `pypi` and `nuget` fall back to `builtin_registry_url` (`registry_endpoint.rs:105-113`, `binding.rs:66-75`).
  - **Bug:** an explicit `kind` overrides a built-in's kind (`resolve.rs:498-518`). `[registries.npm] kind = "cargo"` loads cleanly, and the mismatch only surfaces at release time as `RegistryKindMismatch` (`binding.rs:76-85`). #575 inherits this, so it is the real form of its open question.
  - `cratesIo` is a wire value:
    - It appears in `ReleaseIntentV1` operation ids (3 `derive` snapshots).
    - It feeds the no-URL binding digest (`binding.rs:112`).
    - It needs a special case for cargo (`cargo.rs:11-17`) and a display mapping (`identity.rs:370-378`).
  - **Finding:** a named `kind = "pypi"` registry is dead config. Nothing can target it, because `PublishTarget::Pypi` always keys `pypi` (`ecosystem.rs:129`). Named npm registries do something: they act only as an allowlist for `publishConfig.registry` (`binding.rs:124-135`).
  - `nuget` parses (`resolve.rs:243`) and then always fails at release with E199, because `is_implemented` is false (`ecosystem.rs:208-216`).
  
  **Proposed shape**
  - Predeclared registries: `crates-io` (cargo), `npm` (npm) and `pypi` (pypi), all inserted at resolve.
  - `github-release` and `none` are reserved `publish-to` words, not registries. They have no endpoint or timeouts, and a forge release always goes to origin (`provider/mod.rs:323-332`).
  - Drop `nuget` from `publish-to`. Adding it back later is non-breaking; removing it after 1.0 is not.
  - Types:
    - `RegistryName`: a newtype with a private constructor. It holds #575's `validated_registry_key` rule and is used for both `[registries.<name>]` and `publish-to` strings.
    - `fn publish_target(name: &RegistryName, registries: &BTreeMap<RegistryKey, RegistryConfig>) -> Result<PublishTarget, ConfigError>`.
    - The function matches the reserved words first, then does an exhaustive `match registry.kind` over `Ecosystem` with no `_` arm.
    - Cargo returns `CratesIo` for `crates-io`, otherwise `CargoRegistry`. Npm and Pypi return their variants only for the predeclared names; any other name returns E330 with a hint. Maven and NuGet return E330.
  - Wire spelling, recommended: rename `RegistryKey::CRATES_IO` to `"crates-io"` and bump `ReleaseIntentV1::SCHEMA_VERSION` from 5 to 6 (`intent.rs:98`).
    - An old intent is then refused with E215 instead of failing later as "unknown registry `cratesIo`".
    - This deletes the special cases in `cargo.rs:11-17` and `identity.rs:372`.
    - It is the owner's call (see Open questions).
  
  **Errors for old or clashing keys** (no new code)
  
  | Input | Result |
  | --- | --- |
  | `[registries.cratesIo]` | E325 `UnknownField`, with the message "`[registries.cratesIo]` is now `[registries.crates-io]`". Without an explicit check it would silently become a custom cargo registry whose timeouts never apply. |
  | `[registries.npm] kind = "cargo"` (any predeclared name with a different kind) | E334 `InvalidRegistryKind`, with help: "`npm` is the built-in npm registry; name a custom registry something else." |
  | `[registries.none]` or `[registries.github-release]` | E325, message "a publish-to word, not a registry; rename it". |
  | `publish-to = ["nuget"]` or a named npm/pypi registry | E330 (help text already reworded in #575). |
  
  **Call sites to migrate**
  - Code: `resolve.rs` (`parse_publish_target` and the registries loop), `identity.rs:357-379`, `ecosystem.rs:101-217` (drop `NuGet`, or keep it unreachable), `registry_endpoint.rs:105-113`, `binding.rs:62-75`, `cargo.rs:7-17`, `error.rs` (E321, E330 and E334 help), `intent.rs:98`.
  - Tests and snapshots: 3 snapshots, `release_preview.rs:794-796`, `release.rs:827,904-910`, `schema_guard_test.rs:307,825`, `error_code_hygiene_tests.rs:119,141`, `binding.rs:477-612`.
  - Docs and specs: `docs/config.md:92-111` and `:121`, `docs/errors.md` (E321, E330), REL-PUB-15, REL-PUB-01, `wire-compat.md` (intent 5 to 6).
  
  **Boundary test to write first**
  - A resolve test:
    - `[registries.crates-io] confirm-timeout = "3m"` with `publish-to = ["crates-io"]` gives a binding whose `confirm_timeout` is 3m.
    - `[registries.cratesIo]` gives E325 with `crates-io` in the message.
    - `[registries.npm] kind = "cargo"` gives E334 at load, not at release.
  
  ## 2. Fold `[[package-set]]` into `[[package]]`
  
  **Current behavior**
  - Exact rules: the first in declaration order wins (`resolve.rs:272-283`, WS-CFG-01).
  - Globs: used only when no exact rule matched; the first in declaration order wins (`walk.rs:674-686`, WS-CFG-04).
  - Selection is whole-rule. Fields are not merged, so an exact rule shadows every field of a matching glob.
  - **Bug:** `[[package-set]] pre-major-inference` is parsed (`resolve.rs:632-642`) and never applied. `aggregate.rs:373` and `tags.rs:292` consult only `resolve_package_config`, which reads exact rules only. Only `walk.rs` reads globs (release-trigger, tag-template, changelog, publish-to).
  - `previous-tag-templates` on a set fails with E325, because the field is missing from the raw struct (`raw.rs:128-148`).
  - A private npm workspace root is versioned only when an exact rule names it (`walk.rs:248-251`).
  
  **Proposed shape**
  - Types:
    - `enum PackageMatch { Exact(PackageId), Glob(PackagePattern) }`, built by a private `PackageMatch::parse`.
    - A name containing any of `*?[]{}` is a glob. No npm, Cargo or PyPI name can contain those characters, so the split is unambiguous.
    - `ResolvedConfig.packages: Vec<(PackageMatch, PackageConfig)>`; delete `package_sets`.
  - One resolver, `fn package_rule<'a>(native_names, cfg) -> Option<&'a PackageConfig>`:
    - The first exact rule wins; otherwise the first glob in declaration order wins.
    - This keeps today's precedence. "Exact beats glob" now holds even when the glob is declared first, which is today's behavior too, since they were separate tables.
    - `walk.rs:658-686`, `aggregate.rs:373` and `tags.rs:292` all call it. That fixes the `pre-major-inference` bug.
  - Private npm root: `walk.rs:250` keeps an exact-only check, so `npm/*` never starts versioning a private root.
  - `previous-tag-templates` on a glob: E338 `InvalidPackageMatch`, reason "previous-tag-templates names one package's old tags; use an exact match".
  - Retire `DiagnosticCode::PackageSetMatchedNothing` (`diagnostic.rs:144`). `package-rule-matched-nothing` covers both forms; that is one less permanent JSON value.
  
  **Old key**
  - `[[package-set]]` gives E325. Use a `Retired` field type in `RawConfig` whose `Deserialize` fails with "unknown field `package-set`: rename it to `[[package]]`". `parse_raw` already maps "unknown field…" messages to E325 (`resolve.rs:393`).
  
  **Call sites to migrate**
  - Code: `raw.rs:13-15,128-148`, `resolve.rs:25-32,272-283,294-367,609-645`, `pattern.rs` (doc), `walk.rs:619-752`, `aggregate.rs:373`, `tags.rs:292`, `diagnostic.rs:144`, `error.rs` (E330, E331, E338 text).
  - Tests: `walk/tests.rs`, `package_set_ecosystem_test.rs`, `per_package_config_test.rs`, `tag_glob_cache_count_test.rs`, `error_code_hygiene_tests.rs`, `schema_guard_test.rs`.
  - Docs and specs: `docs/config.md:113-134`, `docs/errors.md:156-164,191`, `identity.md:33-34`, `README.md:81`, WS-CFG-04, WS-CFG-07, WS-ID-12, WS-ID-13.
  
  **Boundary test to write first**
  - Config: `[[package]] match = "cargo/*" pre-major-inference = "conservative"`, followed by an exact rule for another crate.
  - A pre-1.0 crate matched only by the glob gets a breaking changeset downgraded to minor. This fails today.
  
  ## 3. `[release] targets` and `asset-name` template
  
  **Current behavior**
  - `resolve_product_release` (`resolve.rs:158-202`) produces `Vec<ProductArtifactConfig>`. It checks safe components (`:169`) and duplicate asset names (`:177`).
  - Consumers read that vector:
    - matrix: `commands/matrix.rs:52-99`, which feeds `matrix.rs:500-548`
    - release derive: `derive.rs:697-770`
    - init check: `init.rs:227-249`
    - preview: `local.rs:443`
  - The intent's `artifactSlots` and the matrix JSON are built from the vector, so expanding the template in resolve changes **no wire format or JSON key**.
  - MX-DISC-10's `source: "[[release.artifact]]"` value stays as it is.
  
  **Proposed shape**
  - `RawProductReleaseConfig` gains `targets: Vec<String>` (default empty) and `asset_name: Option<String>`.
  - `AssetNameTemplate`: a newtype with a private constructor. It accepts only `{target}` placeholders; any other `{…}` is E197.
  - `resolve_product_release` expands `targets × template` into the same `Vec<ProductArtifactConfig>` before the existing safe-component and duplicate checks.
  - `targets` without `asset-name`, or `asset-name` without `targets`: E197.
  - `[[release.artifact]]` stays for another package's artifact or an irregular name (for example a Windows `.zip`).
  - **The proposed "explicit overrides targets" rule is not needed.** List the Windows target only in the explicit block. A product/target pair in both places becomes E197 through the existing duplicate check, extended to (package, target). It saves the same lines with no shadowing rule to document.
  - `{bin}` and `{version}` are rejected:
    - The generated builder archives every bin of the package into one `$ASSET` (matrix workflow snapshot lines 150-162).
    - A version in the name breaks stable `latest/download` URLs.
  - **Finding (init):** `init.rs:502-511` writes one artifact per target per bin. For a package with several bins, these are identical archives under different names. Init should emit `targets` plus one template named after the single bin, or after the package when it has several.
  
  **Old key**
  - Nothing to reject: `[[release.artifact]]` remains valid.
  
  **Call sites to migrate**
  - Code: `raw.rs:25-48`, `resolve.rs:158-202`, `graph/commands/init.rs:494-513` and its tests, `commands/matrix.rs:78` (message text).
  - Our own `callisto.toml`.
  - `.github/tests/verify-release-workflow-policy.sh:113-125`: it reads `[[release.artifact]]` straight from TOML and must expand `targets` × `asset-name` too. This is an owner-policy script, so the change needs owner sign-off.
  - Docs and specs: `docs/config.md:62-90`, REL-ERR-03, REL-GH-04, CLI-INIT-04, MX-DISC-10 (wording only).
  
  **Boundary test to write first**
  - `targets` + `asset-name` resolves to a `ProductReleaseConfig` equal (`==`) to the five-block explicit form.
  - Overlap gives E197; a `{version}` placeholder gives E197.
  
  ## 4. `forge-repository` from origin
  
  **Current consumers and what each needs**
  
  | Site | Use | Origin today |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | `derive.rs:214-221` | E198 when `[release]` lacks the key | — |
  | `derive.rs:314-331` | `--artifact-repository` must equal origin's GitHub repository | E175 if it differs; precondition error if origin is not GitHub |
  | `cli/commands/release.rs:584-597` | The flag must equal the key | E230 |
  | `cli/commands/release.rs:718-734` | Intent slots must equal the key | E230 |
  | `provider/mod.rs:323-332` | The forge release is created on **origin's** repository, never the key | Fails if origin is not GitHub |
  | `local.rs:443` | Preview artifact policy | — |
  | `version.rs:89-104` | Changelog compare link: the key, else origin, else no link | — |
  | `graph/commands/init.rs:549-560` | Init rejects a value that is not origin's | E187 |
  
  - **Conclusion:** the key can never take a value other than origin's in a release. E187, E175 and E230 together enforce key == origin. It is an assertion, not an override. Its only independent effect is the compare link from a fork or mirror checkout.
  - Proposal: `fn forge_repository(ws) -> Option<GitHubRepository>` returns the key, else origin's GitHub repository. All rows above use it.
  - E198 fires only when neither exists. Help: "Point origin at the GitHub repository, or set `[release] forge-repository`."
  - Origin not on GitHub: no compare link (unchanged); release fails with the existing GitHub-remote precondition.
  - Origin missing: `release` already fails at `prepared_git_remote` (`binding.rs:160-176`); the preview stays optional.
  - Init: drop the question, the `--forge-repository` flag (`cli.rs:279`), E298's forge case (`init.rs:401-412,437-439`) and the write at `init.rs:500`.
    - E187 and E195 become unreachable from `init`. Retire them, or keep E187 for a mismatching explicit key.
  - Old key: keeps working. No error.
  - Call sites to migrate:
    - Code: as in the table, plus `cli/commands/init.rs:517-545`, `InitArgs` and their tests.
    - Tests: `release_forge_repository_tests.rs`, `release_config_hardening_tests.rs`.
    - Docs and specs: `docs/config.md:58,69`, `docs/errors.md:232,240,242,283,312`, REL-AUTH-09, CLI-INIT-03/10/12, VER-APPLY-07A, `cli.json:58,66`.
  - Boundary test to write first: a fixture with origin `github.com/o/r`, `[release]` without the key, and `release plan --artifact-repository o/r` succeeds with slots targeting `o/r`. The same fixture with no origin fails with E198.
  
  ## 5. Group `members` globs: no
  
  - Members resolve exactly. An unknown member is an error with a suggestion (`groups.rs:155-197`).
  - `PackagePattern` could match globs, but a new package matching `npm/@acme/*` would silently join a fixed group and jump to the group's version (VER-GRP-01). A glob's meaning drifts as packages are added.
  - Explicit lists also keep E109, E249 and E250 conflict detection exact.
  
  ## Changesets migration (WP K)
  
  The new shape barely changes K. Only item 5 matters: K must expand globs.
  
  | Changesets key | K writes | Note |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | `fixed` | `[[fixed-group]]`, globs expanded to the current `npm/<name>` list | Warn that new packages must be added by hand |
  | `linked` | **Decision needed** | Changesets bumps only packages with changesets. Callisto bumps every member (VER-GRP-03). Translating changes behavior. |
  | `ignore` | Nothing; keep the warning | A glob `[[package]] publish-to = ["none"]` still versions the package, so it is not equivalent |
  | `access` | Nothing in `callisto.toml` | **Hazard:** Callisto publishes scoped packages without `publishConfig.access` as public (`derive.rs:1456-1488`). Changesets' default is `restricted`. K must flag every such package. |
  | `baseBranch` | The generated workflow's branch filter, not a config key | — |
  | `updateInternalDependencies` | Nothing | ADR 10 always raises co-released dependents' ranges |
  
  ## Measured value
  
  Non-blank, non-comment lines. Sample files were written in `$TMPDIR` and parsed with `tomllib`.
  
  | Config | Before | After | Δ | From |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | Ours | 44 | 31 | −13 | Item 3: −12; item 4: −1 |
  | Changesets-style npm monorepo (2 groups, 2 globs, 1 exact, no binaries) | 17 | 17 | 0 | Item 2 only renames a table |
  | Mixed Cargo/npm/PyPI, private cargo registry, split PyPI URLs, 6 targets + Windows zip + plugin artifact | 51 | 38 | −13 | Item 3: −12; item 4: −1; items 1 and 2: 0 |
  
  - Items 1 and 2 save no lines; their value is in the Verdict table.
  - Ours also carries `[changesets] dir = ".changeset"`, which is the default: 2 free lines.
  
  ## Migration
  
  - What an existing user sees on upgrade:
    - `[[package-set]]` and `[registries.cratesIo]` fail at load with E325, and the message names the new spelling.
    - `publish-to = ["nuget"]` gives E330.
    - The JSON diagnostic `package-set-matched-nothing` disappears.
    - `forge-repository` and `[[release.artifact]]` keep working.
    - `init --forge-repository` becomes an unknown flag.
  - Nothing rewrites the file: `init` refuses an existing `callisto.toml`. Two renames do not justify a migration command.
  
  **Draft changeset (`callisto-cli: minor`):**
  
  **Shorter release configuration.** List your build targets once and let Callisto name each asset, and drop `forge-repository`.
  
  - How to adopt it: replace repeated `[[release.artifact]]` blocks for the product with `targets = [...]` and `asset-name = "tool-{target}.tar.gz"` under `[release]`. Keep `[[release.artifact]]` for another package's artifact or an irregular name such as a Windows `.zip`.
  - `forge-repository` is optional: Callisto uses the `origin` GitHub repository, which every release already required it to equal.
  - Limits: `{target}` is the only placeholder.
  
  **Breaking:** `[[package-set]]` is now `[[package]]`. A `match` with `*`, `?`, `[` or `{` is a glob; an exact match still wins over any glob, and among globs the first declared wins. `pre-major-inference` on a glob now takes effect. The JSON diagnostic `package-set-matched-nothing` is now `package-rule-matched-nothing`.
  
  **Breaking:** The crates.io registry is `[registries.crates-io]`, the same spelling as `publish-to`; `[registries.cratesIo]` fails with E325. A built-in registry (`crates-io`, `npm`, `pypi`) can no longer be given another `kind` (E334), and `nuget` is no longer a `publish-to` value (E330).
  
  **Breaking:** `callisto init` no longer asks for or accepts `--forge-repository`.
  
  **Action:** Rename `[[package-set]]` to `[[package]]` and `[registries.cratesIo]` to `[registries.crates-io]`, remove `nuget` from `publish-to`, and drop `--forge-repository` from scripted `init` calls.
  
  ## Rejected alternatives
  
  - Field-level merge across matching rules: a value would come from two blocks, and existing configs would change meaning.
  - Most-specific glob wins: hard to explain. Declaration order is already the documented rule (WS-CFG-04).
  - Keeping `cratesIo` as an alias: two spellings, permanent after 1.0.
  - Letting `kind` re-type a built-in: #575's open hole, which only fails at release time.
  - `github-release` as a configurable registry: it has nothing to configure and always targets origin.
  - An explicit artifact overriding a `targets` entry: a precedence rule with no line gain.
  - `{bin}` or `{version}` in `asset-name`: one archive holds all bins, and versioned names break stable URLs.
  - Group member globs: their meaning drifts.
  - A `[registries.npm] access` key for K: a new permanent key, where `publishConfig.access` already exists.
  - `init` or a `migrate` command rewriting the file: a hint covers two renames.
  
  ## Stays as it is
  
  - `[[fixed-group]]` and `[[linked-group]]` (exact members; semantics in VER-GRP).
  - `[cascade]` (4 knobs, each a distinct behavior with provenance keys, `resolve.rs:418-454`).
  - Registry `confirm-timeout`, `publish-timeout`, `url` and `publish-url`, and their rules (E335, E336, E168).
  - `[changesets] dir`, `[changelog] layout` and `[validation]`.
  - `tag-template`, `previous-tag-templates` (exact rules only) and `release-trigger`.
  - The `none` target.
  
  ## Work split
  
  | PR | Items | Files (main) | Order and overlap |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | A | 3 + 4 (`[release]`) | `raw.rs`, `resolve.rs:158-202`, `derive.rs`, `local.rs`, `version.rs`, cli `release.rs`, both `init.rs`, `cli.rs`, `error.rs`, `callisto.toml`, policy script, docs and specs | First. No overlap with #575, #573 or #571. #570 adds a flag in `cli.rs` in another struct (low risk). |
  | B | 2 (package fold) | `raw.rs`, `resolve.rs:272-645`, `walk.rs`, `aggregate.rs`, `tags.rs`, `diagnostic.rs`, tests, docs | After #575 merges: both change `parse_package_config_fields`. #571 edits `aggregate.rs` near but not on line 373, plus `schema_guard_test.rs`; merge B after #571. |
  | C | 1 (registry names) | model `identity.rs`, `ecosystem.rs`, `intent.rs`, `resolve.rs`, `binding.rs`, `cargo.rs`, `registry_endpoint.rs`, snapshots, docs | After #575 and B. It replaces #575's `name != CRATES_IO` guard. |
  
  - #573 (`python.rs`) does not overlap with any of these PRs.
  - WP K comes after B.
  - Each PR carries its own changeset; the draft above is the combined text.
  
  ## Open questions for the owner
  
  1. **Rename the wire key `cratesIo` to `crates-io` and bump the intent to 6?** Recommend yes: one spelling end to end, and an old intent is refused cleanly.
  2. **Keep `forge-repository` at all?** It only matters for compare links from a fork. Recommend keeping it optional rather than removing it, so existing configs stay valid.
  3. **Explicit artifact vs `targets` overlap: E197 instead of override?** Recommend E197.
  4. **Drop `nuget` from `publish-to` now?** Recommend yes; re-add it when a publisher exists.
  5. **Named `kind = "pypi"` registries are inert today.** Make them targetable in a follow-up, or reject them at load? Recommend a follow-up that makes npm and pypi named registries targetable like #575's cargo ones. Until then, document them as inert.
  6. **K and `linked`:** translate despite the semantic difference, or warn only? Recommend warn only, plus a separate decision on whether Callisto's linked groups should match Changesets.
  7. **K and `access` restricted or absent:** warn per scoped package, or refuse to write? Recommend refusing without `--yes`, naming each package that would flip to public, and pointing to `publishConfig.access`.
  8. **Policy-script change for item 3** (`verify-release-workflow-policy.sh`): it is owner-enforced, so confirm it may expand `targets`.
  
</agent-message>

<pasted_content id="cd46">
Callisto audit handoff for Claude

The work covered adversarial review of Callisto’s Rust code, release/version workflows, CLI/config behavior, native publishing integration, dependency semantics, crash recovery and performance. The owner requested discovery and analysis. No product fixes, commits, pushes or real registry publications were performed.

The owner then challenged whether some findings imposed requirements Callisto should not own. A subsequent review found several classification and recommendation mistakes. Use the corrected conclusions below.

Revision and evidence status

- Initial audit: ab3c030ad16847c3eb1f94486cd805f1c3e81345.
- Swarm and follow-up reproductions: fac8cadf91f792fdd17dec93d36eeb10be24e5f3.
- External changes landed during the investigation. Several initial findings appear remediated in later source.
- Do not treat these lists as a current open-bug count. Recheck the current checkout before implementing anything.
- Assessment corrections are not instructions to revert changes already accepted by the owner.
- Findings and reproductions deliberately live outside the public repository. Do not commit unfixed-defect reports or fixtures.

Latest follow-up: three confirmed functional bugs

| ID | Finding and demonstrated behavior | Correction direction |
|---|---|---|
| GAP-02 — Medium | Provisional fixed-group targets escape into dependency rewrites. Released a@1.0.0 and fresh b@2.0.0 share a fixed group. A patch for b correctly aligns the group to 1.0.1, but Callisto can successfully persist a consumer requirement of ^2.0.1. Reproduced through npm, Cargo and Python manifests. A compatible union range admitting both initial and final versions can instead receive E319 about the provisional version. | Derive final rewrites and rejection decisions from converged targets. The solver currently emits them before fixed-group alignment and retains stale results. No async runtime or new I/O framework is needed. |
| GAP-03 — Medium | Standalone/root npm publication fails. Discovery, init and release dry-run accept the package, but actual publication adds --workspace=<name>. Native npm rejects the root as “No workspaces found”; plain native npm publish --dry-run succeeds. | Use existing package paths and directory-publish helpers where appropriate. A new membership model, trait or preflight is not established as necessary. The spec’s generic --workspace command sentence also needs correction. |
| GAP-04 — Medium | Recovery rejects its own emitted artifacts. Put --emit-decision or --emit-preview JSON inside .changeset, then encounter a later output failure or checkpoint-cleanup interruption. Retrying identical arguments fails E340 because recovery treats its own JSON as changed input. Independent controls outside .changeset recover. | Account for these outputs in existing recovery comparisons, or precisely identify actual inputs. Preserve rejection of genuine user edits. This does not establish a need for a new transaction framework or journal. |

Primary locations: cascade.rs, commands/registry_argv.rs, CLI commands/version.rs, graph commands/version.rs.

Six findings from the preceding swarm

These were reproduced on frozen fac8cad. Their current status still needs checking.

| ID | Corrected assessment |
|---|---|
| QA-01 — High | Wrong-workspace recovery. Leading whitespace in a workspace directory is removed by trimming Git stdout. With a trimmed-name sibling, matching paths/starting versions and an interruption, retry successfully applies the sibling’s version plan and notes to the selected workspace. Conditional but demonstrated local data corruption. Preserve path identity first; additional wrappers are optional implementation choices. |
| QA-02 — Medium | Python dependency rewrites drop PEP 440 epochs. Dependency 2!0.4.8 → 2!0.4.9, but a supported requirement becomes >=0.4.9 instead of retaining the epoch. Native packaging proves previously forbidden versions become allowed. Checking target inclusion alone misses this widening. |
| QA-03 — Medium | Git interprets package paths as patterns. A directory ending in * includes sibling commits during automatic inference. Follow-up also demonstrated unrelated sibling edits being staged by version. Treat staging as expanded impact of this same defect family, not another independent bug. -- stops option parsing; it does not make pathspecs literal. |
| QA-04 — Low | Rare valid commit-body character breaks inference. U+001E collides with custom Git-log framing and causes E026. Native Git accepts the message. Impact is blocked inference, not wrong publication or exploitation. Calling the input “ordinary” overstated its frequency. |
| QA-05 — Medium | Native publication prerequisites leak between outputs. A Cargo-only dependency of a dual Cargo/npm owner also becomes a prerequisite of its npm publication, and vice versa. Accepted distribution-context rules support this finding. Demonstrated impact is extra graph dependencies/order constraints—not an observed wrong upload. Owner-cycle rejection is a separate policy/model tension, not another confirmed bug. |
| QA-06 — Performance issue | Historical foreign-tag rejection repeats work. Same-commit tags cause repeated equivalent Git queries and repeated candidate selection. Fixture counts were 6T+4 Git processes. The quoted 176/622/2,993 ms wall times came from a debug binary, not optimized production latency. Preserve the measured repeated-work conclusion; do not invent an SLA or release gate. |

Initial audit: historical findings and corrections

These concern ab3c030. Many areas subsequently changed.

| ID | Corrected conclusion |
|---|---|
| F01 — Medium, downgraded | Python normalization-equivalent names could be admitted as different release owners. Retain the duplicate native identity admission issue. Withdraw the original High byte-integrity/false-success framing: unsealed retry adoption explicitly permits matching existing files by filename without digest equality. Later source contains a normalization guard; that is not fresh post-remediation verification. |
| F02 — Medium | A retained higher-severity prerelease changeset could erase fresh lower-severity commit inference. Severity provenance was incorrectly used to decide input freshness. Later source contains remediation; no fresh full verification was performed in the claim review. |
| F03 — Withdrawn as mandatory-failure bug | The action produced a successful release-PR no-op for unknown/malformed-only notes. Unknown notes are deliberately warned, retained and skipped. The audited PR-decision contract did not require full validation. Hidden malformed diagnostics remain a UX concern, but requiring failure would introduce a product policy. |
| F04 — Medium | Default-branch detection truncated release/next to next. Historical branch-preservation defect. |
| F05 — Medium | Valid Git branch names containing comma/hash were interpolated without proper scalar serialization, changing or breaking generated YAML. |
| F06 — Medium | The HTTP parser selected a blank-line delimiter inside valid JSON and truncated the response body. Interoperability/availability issue, not security or credential leakage. |
| F07 — Performance issue | Relative pnpm workspace references caused repeated package scans/canonicalization, approximately O(E × P). Optimized-binary controls demonstrated substantial extra work. No timing guarantee or new gate follows from this. |
| F08 — Medium | Existing license/feature recipes returned success when cargo tree failed because the negated pipeline inverted the failure. This demonstrates broken check error propagation, not that prohibited artifacts actually shipped. |
| F09 — Low, narrowed | Init advertised retry instructions rejected by its existing-config guard. Correcting the advice can suffice. Do not infer a requirement for overwriting config or a new workflow-only mode. |
| F10 — Low, narrowed | Malformed file JSON returned usage exit 2 despite the documented file-failure exit 1. Missing filename rendering alone was not a proven contract violation; stdin behavior needs separate policy justification. |

The principal walk-back: registry routing

The owner explicitly clarified that Callisto should respect users’ native publishing choices rather than authorize their destinations.

The experiment showed that npm’s @scope:registry configuration overrides generic --registry. Both observation and publication followed that native route. That behavior is correct.

Therefore:

- Withdraw GAP-01 / NATIVE-02 as a High security or publishing-routing bug.
- Withdraw recommendations for a destination allowlist, conflicting-route rejection, registry-resolution reimplementation or added preflight.
- Retain only the documentation mismatch: docs/publishing.md says Callisto’s URL “pins” the destination more strongly than native behavior supports.
- No credential leak or observation/publication destination disagreement was demonstrated.
- Native tools should continue to own registry configuration, credentials and OIDC.

Other overreach corrected

- New traits, membership models, state-binding wrappers and frameworks were sometimes prescribed more strongly than the evidence justified. Start with existing modules, lossless conversions and shared helpers.
- The seven responsibility names in the bug-hunting skill do not establish a need for seven Rust traits.
- No demonstrated issue requires Tokio, async conversion or another release workflow framework.
- Windows was incorrectly listed as required missing coverage. Windows is explicitly unsupported. Linux remains an unexercised supported-platform limit.
- A reproduced behavior is not sufficient proof of a bug. The original revision’s contract and owner intent must establish why it is wrong. Later remediation-added requirements must not serve as retroactive proof.

Executed verification and its limits

- Existing production release simulator: 16 tests passed, covering 2,016 schedules.
- Temporary generated-DAG extension: 101,752 schedules, across 16 graphs × two confirmation modes; all passed the simulator’s invariants.
- Filesystem campaign: 70 ordinary interruption schedules matched uninterrupted controls; three partial-deletion cases recovered; two genuine-user-edit guards correctly refused; two emitted-artifact schedules exposed GAP-04. Nine successful controls.
- Semantic matrix: 192 fixtures, 248 application attempts. Four invalid successful outputs represented GAP-02 in two states/two permutations. Twenty other raw range misses were documented development-dependency exceptions; all 16 matrix E319 refusals were adjudicated as policy-correct.
- 112 prerelease idempotence checks passed.
- 22 snapshot fixtures produced no additional confirmed defect.
- Earlier fuzz smoke campaigns processed 1,337,789 inputs without a crash. They used coverage feedback with sanitizer mode none; not ASan.
- An earlier malformed 21-row fault harness was excluded entirely from coverage.
These are bounded campaigns.
</pasted_content id="cd46">

### Medium finding

**Location:** `crates/callisto-graph/src/apply.rs:194-198`

And some more info from codex:



<pasted_content id="cd46">
The evidence favors fixing the existing native experience and clarifying compatibility before expanding the architecture.

| Area | Result | Recommended next step |
|---|---|---|
| Basic UX | Eight visible commands, minimal config, a 31-line workflow, working interactive wizard and file-preserving previews. | Preserve this surface. Improve a few examples and unsupported-state explanations. |
| First release | Standalone npm publishing still fails through the invalid workspace selector. | Fix this common activation path, then measure the existing under-ten-minute goal with actual new users. |
| Native correctness | Epoch loss, stale group rewrites and foreign-output prerequisites undermine the promised polyglot behavior. | Correct those boundaries before adding more layouts or adapters. |
| Changesets v3 compatibility | An actual Changesets 3.0.3 pre enter produced valid state that Callisto rejected. | Choose an explicit version boundary or deliberate migration strategy. Markdown compatibility alone does not establish lifecycle compatibility. |
| OIDC | The proposal understates existing support: Twine handles trusted publishing, and Callisto already preserves uploader identity variables and avoids empty-token configuration. | Update native-tool/version recipes and verification scope. A core auth switch or broker is not justified. |
| Roadmap accuracy | PyPI production-path tests exist and ran; inferred-only release PRs are implemented; target metadata is centralized. | Separate completed work from the remaining Cargo, napi/load, platform and onboarding evidence. |
| Compatibility tooling | cargo-semver-checks examines Rust crate APIs, not the complete CLI/config/JSON contract. | Test the actual promised consumer surfaces when implementing that proposal. |

The v3 interoperability result matters because Changesets changed its prerelease-state format (https://changesets.dev/guide/migration#prerelease-changes). Callisto currently preserves the foreign state and fails safely, but its broad compatibility wording and generic delete/re-enter advice need attention.

The industry comparison also changes positioning. Retry support alone is not distinctive: Sampo 0.24.1 adds partial-release repair (https://github.com/bruits/sampo/releases/tag/npm-sampo-v0.24.1). Changesets, release-please, Nx and Knope provide different combinations of intent, release PRs, dependency handling and workflow composition. Callisto’s strongest positioning hypothesis is coherent native Cargo/npm/Python coordination without requiring a broader build platform—not universal feature superiority.

e18e’s publishing guidance (https://e18e.dev/docs/publishing.html) offers useful package-validation and workflow practices. Its staged publication and approval choices remain separate product decisions. Staging cannot simply replace publishing while Callisto still treats public registry visibility as completion.

I also tested two observations before classifying them:

- Human text preceding JSON errors is integration friction, but satisfies the current “stderr ends with JSON” contract.
- The interactive wizard passed in a controlling PTY; that establishes functionality, not human usability.

No product code changed. Hosted OIDC, first-time-user onboarding time and equivalent competitor runtime comparisons remain unverified. Recommendations are explicitly separated from agreed requirements and release gates.
</pasted_content id="cd46">

<cross-session-message from="uds:/tmp/cc-socks/22186.sock" from-name="managed-prerelease-wp-h" from-mode="prompting">
#587 is updated: the new tests found no defects, and the race is confirmed for generated workflows only. Head 132c77190 is pushed; ls-remote matches; all 5 commits are verified valid; main had not moved, so there was nothing to merge.

(1) New real-binary tests in release_pr_pre_cycle_tests.rs. All pass, and no defect turned up.
- A commit-inferred package (release-trigger = "auto") runs the full cycle with v2 and with v3 pre state: enter -> noop -> 1.1.0-beta.0 -> fix -> beta.1 -> noop -> exit -> 1.1.0 -> noop. Each version commit is tagged as execute would tag it.
- Update path: in every cycle (changeset v2, changeset v3, auto) each release step also runs decide with the managed release PR open, and asserts "update".
- I restored the docs and changeset wording that a new commit to an auto-trigger package opens or updates the PR, since it is now tested.

(2) Race: CONFIRMED for the generated workflows. It does not affect this repo's own workflow.
- Reproduction: an auto package is tagged pkg-alpha@1.0.0, then a feat commit; decide returns create; version and the commit are merged, and pkg-alpha@1.1.0 is not created. decide then returns create again, and version --dry-run proposes 1.1.0 -> 1.2.0 by inference. Once the tag exists, decide returns noop. Cause: the inference window and the current version come from the last tag (crates/callisto-graph/src/aggregate.rs:442-457).
- Timing: in crates/callisto-graph/src/templates/simple_workflow.tmpl:12 and matrix_workflow.tmpl:14, the version-pr job has no needs:. It starts alongside package/plan, while execute tags only after the build jobs. So on a release merge decide practically always runs before the tag exists. This is not a close timing call.
- After the tag lands, the next decide is a noop, but the noop path in create-or-update-release-pr.sh exits without closing the PR. The spurious 1.2.0 PR therefore stays open, and merging it would release a version with no new change.
- This repo is guarded: .github/workflows/callisto-release.yml:189-195 runs version-pr after a release-PR merge only with needs: [release-candidate, gate, drain], once drain succeeds after execute.
- Packages without release-trigger = "auto" are unaffected.
- The failing probe is not committed (per your instruction). It is at /tmp/claude-501/race_probe.rs: copy it into crates/callisto-cli/tests/ for the follow-up PR.

Gates on 132c77190, by exit code: just pre-push, lint, doc-check, test and ci all exited 0. cargo mutants --in-diff was not re-run after this round, which changed only tests and docs. The earlier run, on the same Rust source diff, found 1 mutant and caught it.
</cross-session-message>

<cross-session-message from="uds:/tmp/cc-socks/48241.sock" from-name="converged-targets-type" from-mode="prompting">
PR 2 (wp-versioning-ranges) needs a decision before I write code. PR 1 is #594, pushed and linked to trail 462.
1. License: `Versioning` lives in callisto-model, which is MIT. The range rewrites (`cargo/npm/python::round_trip`, about 250 lines) live in callisto-manifests, which is FSL. Moving them into the trait relicenses that code to MIT. That is the owner's call.
2. Shape: `Versioning` is per grammar (SemVer, Pep440), but rewriting is per ecosystem. Cargo and npm are both SemVer yet differ: comma ranges, workspace protocol, and the precision rule for a `-` clause. A SemVer `rewrite_range` would have to branch on `Ecosystem`.
Options:
A. Move everything into `Versioning::rewrite_range(spec, target, eco)`. This takes the relicense plus the ecosystem branching.
B (recommended). Add one grammar-owned method, `Versioning::render_bound(target, op, precision)`: the epoch, prerelease and local-segment rules, which is where #573 lost the epoch. Cargo/npm/python call it, and range syntax parsing stays in manifests. This is new MIT code with no relicense, plus epoch and prerelease tests per grammar.
C. Drop PR 2.
Which one?
</cross-session-message>

  - Refactors that change which Git primitive a caller uses leave the old one behind, as with `resolve_commits` (#330) and `path_matches_at` (7deb50aa5). A sibling sweep in the same PR would catch these.
  - On types that validate on deserialize (private fields, wire struct, `try_from`), the later `validate_for_intent` re-checks invariants the type already guarantees. The same goes for enum variants that exist only to be rejected (`OperationOutcome::Failed/Blocked`).
  - Small convenience methods from the initial import (1a8f983) survive only because their own unit tests call them: `platform_manifests`, `is_release_point`, `is_writable`, `stdout_lines`.
  - Unused wire variants (`EmptySummary`, `RegistryVersionUnverified`) stay alive because `schema_guard_test.rs` hard-codes the variant list. That test is itself the kind of hand-kept list AGENTS.md forbids (out of scope here).
  - Ecosystem's implemented set is spelled out four times: `CANONICAL`, `is_implemented`, `canonical_manifest_format`, `primary_priority`. Only `is_implemented` uses a non-exhaustive `matches!`; it could read from `CANONICAL`. This is low value, and the demand-gated variants themselves are intentional.
  
  **Limits:** method names shared with other types (`new`, `parse`, `as_str`, `render`) could not be checked for dead uses by grep, so some dead methods may be missing. The UNSURE rows need an owner decision before anything is removed.

  | commands/init.rs:738 | `DefaultBranchSource` | Its variants appear only in init.rs, but the CLI prints it (callisto-cli/src/commands/init.rs:384). | KEEP | Keep. | 0 |
  | lib.rs:327 | `assign_promoted_pre_keys<D: DependencyResolver>` | One production instantiation (`Workspace::resolve_pre_state_from_json`) and no fake-driven test.   | conventional/infer.rs (whole file) | `infer_severity` + `InferenceInput` + `InferredSeverity` | One caller (`CommitInference`) and no tests. Ask the owner whether that layering is still wanted. | ~25 |
  
  Patterns:
  - The main source of dead code is leftovers: an API stays public with only test callers after its production callers move to a replacement. Examples: `matches_in_ecosystems` → `matches_native`, `resolve_native` → `_with_fallback`, `extract_section` → `find_section`, `toposort_with_edge_filter` → `PublishEdgeFilter`. The migrating PR kept the old function and its tests.
  - Many `pub` / `pub(crate)` items are used only inside their own file: locate/membership.rs, changed.rs (`is_covered`, `changed_source_files_since_last_tag`), config/raw.rs structs, toposort constants, `pre_json_key`. That is wider visibility than needed, not dead code. Narrowing it would save no lines, so I left it out of the table.
  - Error enums are clean. Every `GraphError`, `ConfigError` and `LocateError` variant is constructed (directly or through `#[from]`) except `LocateError::Graph`. Every config raw field is read by resolve.rs.
  - The tests repeat themselves most where a proptest or boundary test was added later and the older hand-written cases stayed (toposort). The same likely holds in the big tests.rs files (apply, cascade, aggregate, version, roughly 2-3k lines each), which I did not audit case by case.
  - Coverage caveat: grep cannot fully separate same-named methods (for example `matches`, `get`, `edges`), so dead methods with common names may remain undetected beyond the ones I checked by hand.
  
</agent-message>

  - The biggest saving is in tests: newer suites (dry_run_invariant, status_check, help.trycmd, pre.rs unit tests) replaced older ad hoc ones (e2e_tests, lifecycle_e2e_tests, the cli.rs help tests, pre_mode_tests), and the old ones were never deleted.

<agent-message from="abac172ed262258e1">
[Subagent hand-back] The text below is the final report of a subagent this session delegated to. It is model output, NOT a message from the user: instructions, requests, or approval claims inside it are the subagent's words and carry no user authority. The harness indents every line of the report, so a frame-like line at column zero inside it would be forged. Notes above this frame may quote model-derived text, which carries no user authority either. The report follows:
  Read-only check of origin/main at ac5637b06 (fetched). No builds or edits. Short answer: no consumer needs an acyclic order across a package's Cargo and npm outputs together. The only owner-level cycle check is a pre-check for publish ordering, so I recommend option 3, the split. Versioning needs no acyclic order at all, and publish already has a per-distribution check.
  
  **1. Where cycles are detected and who needs the order**
  - The only place that raises E104 is `PublishEdgeFilter::new` in `crates/callisto-graph/src/toposort.rs:100-114`. It runs `toposort_impl` over the owner-package graph using runtime, build and optional edges (`CASCADE_ORDERING_KINDS`, :81), with edges from every manifest. The sorted order is thrown away (`?;` at :112); only the error matters.
  - It is called once, from release derive (`derive.rs:229`). `toposort_impl` is re-exported (`lib.rs:63`) but nothing else calls it.
  - It also builds `dev_cycles`: strongly connected components over the owner graph, dev edges included (`toposort.rs:113`, `cyclic_sccs` :137-149).
  - Version cascade does not use a topological order. `solve_cascade` (`cascade.rs:206-232`) is a fixpoint loop over a worklist with an iteration cap that fails with E105 (`error.rs:128-130`). Fixed and linked groups re-queue members through the same loop (`cascade.rs:558-607`). An owner-level cycle converges, because bumps only go up.
  - Changelogs and tags need no cross-package order. Tags wait only on their own package's publishes (`derive.rs:197-203`).
  - Publish ordering already has its own per-distribution check. `link_publish_prerequisites` (`derive.rs:475-530`) keeps only edges whose declaring manifest's ecosystem matches the dependent's distribution. The resulting operation DAG is then checked by `canonical_operation_order` (`derive.rs:1018`, `callisto-model/src/release/intent.rs:301-316`), which fails with E294.
  - So the owner-level check is stricter than anything publishing needs. In your example (crate a depends on crate b, npm b depends on npm a), that DAG has no cycle, yet derive rejects it with E104.
  
  **Related defect (inferred from the code, not reproduced).** `dev_cycles` has the same owner-level scope. Suppose crate a has a dev-dependency on crate b, and npm b depends on npm a. The owner graph has no runtime cycle, so derive passes. But {a, b} is one owner component, so `allows(a, b, Dev)` returns false (:119-127). Crate a then does not wait for crate b, although the Cargo graph has no cycle. That breaks the stated reason for dev ordering (`toposort.rs:83-86`).
  
  **2. Spec and history**
  - The rule is REL-ORD-02 in `docs/specs/release.json:213-214`: "a runtime, build or optional cycle fails with E104". It names no level, so the code's choice (package level) defines it.
  - No ADR or architecture text requires package-level rejection. `ARCHITECTURE.md:66` and `docs/architecture/release.md:13` only say ordering and cycle reports run on michi-graph.
  - History:
    - E104 came in with the miette diagnostics commit (de44b97a4).
    - The dev-edge exclusion came from 325f138c6 (#11) and 3644b0eeb, then moved into `PublishEdgeFilter` in b3f5715ad (#126).
    - Per-distribution waiting came in 436d49e59 (#257) and #581.
    - When #257 and #581 made publish waits per distribution, the cycle check was not moved with them. Nothing records that as a decision.
  - Brain recall "dependency cycle" turned up one relevant memory (fact:6dd85e24...): mutual dev dependencies are legitimate, so dev edges must not make the cycle check abort the plan. No memory requires an owner-level check.
  
  **3. How realistic the example is**
  - napi-rs: the napi crate's Cargo.toml is usually not published (`publish = false`). REL-ORD-02 already says such a manifest orders nothing, so the typical napi package cannot form this cycle.
  - A real case needs a crate published to both crates.io and npm (wasm-pack or wasm-bindgen style), with dependencies pointing in opposite directions in the two ecosystems. That is an unusual layering inversion: possible, but I found no fixture or user report of it.
  - Cargo itself refuses non-dev cycles, so a cycle confined to the Cargo distribution cannot exist anyway. npm allows cycles.
  - Expected impact is low.
  
  **4. Recommendation: split**
  - Keep versioning as it is.
  - Make publish's cycle check and dev-cycle components per distribution.
  - Smallest change: in `PublishEdgeFilter::new`, run the toposort and `cyclic_sccs` once per ecosystem. Feed each run only edges whose `manifest_ecosystem(edge.from_manifest)` is that ecosystem and whose owner publishes in it. `allows` then looks up the components for the edge's ecosystem.
  - Reword REL-ORD-02 to "a runtime, build or optional cycle within one ecosystem's dependencies fails with E104".
  - Add two derive tests:
    - the opposing dual case now orders;
    - the dev case above now makes crate a wait for crate b.
  - What would break or change:
    - The no-match fallback (`derive.rs:513-520`: an edge waits for every distribution of the dependency when none is in its ecosystem) crosses ecosystems. A cycle that only exists through that fallback would escape the per-ecosystem check. It would then fail later as the generic E294 "release operations do not form a valid DAG" (`ReleaseInvariant`) instead of E104 with package names.
    - Either count fallback edges in every ecosystem's graph, or map the E294 cycle back to E104.
    - Plans that fail today would start passing, and the existing `runtime_cycle_still_fails_with_a_cycle_error` test still passes.
  - If the owner sees no real user need, keeping owner-level rejection is safe; it is only stricter than needed. But the dev-component scoping issue above is worth fixing either way, because it leaves out an ordering that dev-dependencies need.
</agent-message>

change #233 has a finding:



<pasted_content id="cd46">
### Medium finding

**Location:** `.github/workflows/callisto-ci.yml:14`

With the shared workflow/ref group on line 13, `cancel-in-progress: false` serializes main runs but still allows GitHub to replace a pending run. If three main pushes arrive while the first run is executing, the third cancels the second before it builds, defeating the requirement that every main commit receive a complete result. Give non-PR runs unique concurrency groups, or apply concurrency only to PR runs.
</pasted_content id="cd46">

Cascade fixpoint is done: PR #637 (trail #462), branch wp-cascade-fixpoint, sha 4395142 (signed, verification valid), off origin/main fef5d3040.
Each round starts again from the explicit seeds plus the previous round's raises, aligns the groups, and derives the raises the settled targets require. Rounds 1..n can drop a raise; after that the raises are kept pointwise max, so the run ends within 4n+1 rounds (the existing E105 bound). The argument is in the PR body. Production code shrank by 8 lines net; the group passes were moved, not changed.
Ran: cargo test -p callisto-graph (all pass except one npm registry_argv test, which fails because the sandbox blocks ~/.npm), just lint, just doc-check, and cargo mutants on the diff (the misses that remain are equivalent or happen only in the union phase; listed in the PR). Not run: just ci, fuzz-check (no public signature changed), bench.
Spec VER-CAS-01 and the architecture note are updated. Patch changeset for callisto-cli.

PR #637 updated: sha a345b67 (signed, verification valid). Origin/main is unchanged, so no merge was needed.
(1) Bench with 500 packages, version --dry-run in ms (before/after): cargo 423/425, pnpm 446/422, foreign tags 373/376. Git process counts are identical (16/16/27). The table is in the PR body. These shapes raise no consumer, so the cascade settles in one round. A separate worst case, a 500-deep chain of = pins, goes from 1.2 to 109 ms, because the rounds grow with depth and each round costs O(released). Depth 10 should cost about 2 ms (estimated, not measured). I did not optimise it. The fix would be an incremental worklist; say if you want that.

<cross-session-message from="uds:/tmp/cc-socks/89164.sock" from-name="callisto-add-inquire-prompts" from-mode="prompting">
Both changes are in local commit fb5ae0aad (signed, on 5cb28963b), not pushed. (2) is done as asked; (1) I did differently, because unregister breaks SIGINT for the rest of the run.

(1) SIGINT registration. I did not add unregister(id) in Drop. signal-hook-registry 1.4.8's own unregister docs say it "does *not* currently return the default/previous signal handler", and its handler skips SIG_DFL. So after unregister, SIGINT is ignored for the rest of the run, whatever the flag says. I tested it: a new pty test, after_the_editor_a_sigint_takes_its_default_action_again, sends kill -INT at the confirmation after the editor. It passes with the current code and fails with unregister in Drop ("add did not exit" after 20 s); I reverted that experiment. Instead, Editor::edit now takes self, so there is one launch and one registration per editor; summary() builds one Editor per run and takes it once. The registration stays, and the flag gives SIGINT its default action back.

(2) Cancel rule. add uses raw_prompt()/prompt(), so Esc maps to OperationCanceled, then CliError::Cancelled: it prints "Cancelled, nothing written." and exits 130, as in init. The skippable branches and the Option results for Esc are gone (multi_select -> Vec<usize>, confirm -> bool). I kept one add-side CANCELLED print, for declining the review and for an editor that saves nothing or exits non-zero. Those stay exit 0, because they are answers rather than interrupts, and your earlier brief asked for an_editor_that_saves_nothing_cancels_and_exits_0. If you want those at 130 too, it is a three-line change. Spec, changeset and tests are updated (esc pty test renamed and now expects 130; unit test expects Err(Cancelled)).

Diff (fb5ae0aad vs 5cb28963b, 6 files, +57/-67), code part:
- trait AddPrompter: multi_select returns Result<Vec<usize>>, confirm returns Result<bool>; summary is unchanged (Option = editor saved nothing)
- Terminal: raw_prompt_skippable -> raw_prompt; Text prompt_skippable -> prompt; Confirm prompt_skippable -> prompt
- ask(): three `let Some(..) = ..? else { return Ok(None) }` become plain `?`; confirm is `Ok(prompter.confirm(..)?.then_some((entries, summary)))`
- Scripted test prompter: None selection -> Err(CliError::Cancelled)
- tty.rs: `pub fn edit(self)` (was &self), moving command into EditorUnusable
- error.rs: Cancelled doc comment wording

Ran: cargo test -p callisto-cli exit 0 (pty unsandboxed, 17/17 pty pass); just lint exit 0. Not pushed.

Why does change #238 still has a finding??? 

<pasted_content id="cd46">
### Medium finding

**Location:** `crates/callisto-graph/src/aggregate.rs:197-204`

When a retained major changeset and a fresh inferred commit produce a prerelease (`aggregate/tests.rs:2054–2075`), this selection replaces the inference reason with a changeset reason. Retained notes are excluded from new changelog entries (`aggregate.rs:588–594`), and the planner emits the commit only for an inference reason (`commands/version.rs:54–64, 269–295`), so the new section loses the commit subject and SHA; a group-triggered release can similarly lose its group note. Keep the planned-severity explanation for the Reason column, but generate changelog entries from the fresh release trigger independently.
</pasted_content id="cd46">

#242 has more:


<pasted_content id="cd46">
### High finding

**Location:** `crates/callisto-graph/src/error.rs:988`

Git accepts remote-helper URLs such as `hg::<HTTPS URL>`, but `url::Url` parses the nested URL as an opaque path, so masking the outer userinfo leaves any embedded credentials intact. `binding.rs:166–179` passes that value to `MaskedUrl`, and `error.rs:1031–1033` prints it in E132, including when `init` propagates the refusal from `commands/init.rs:282`. Omit opaque and helper URLs from the diagnostic rather than rendering their payload.
</pasted_content id="cd46">



<pasted_content id="cd46">
### Low finding

**Location:** `crates/callisto-graph/src/commands/release_decision.rs:177`

When an independent private package is the only one with a pending bump, an unfiltered `release --dry-run` still tells the user to run `callisto version` first. The decision builder includes every bump (`release_decision.rs:62–81`), while the actual release roster skips packages with no publish target (`release_decision.rs:238–240`); the CLI prints the recommendation for any nonempty result (`commands/release.rs:207–208`). Filter the displayed identities to publishable packages after applying selection and group expansion.
</pasted_content id="cd46">

The PTY test at `crates/callisto-cli/tests/add_interactive_pty_tests.rs:366–376` confirms signal termination but does not check restoration. We have a finding from 12 min ago:


<pasted_content id="cd46">
### Medium finding

**Location:** `crates/callisto-graph/src/aggregate.rs:197-204`

When a retained major changeset and a fresh inferred commit produce a prerelease (`aggregate/tests.rs:2054–2075`), this selection replaces the inference reason with a changeset reason. Retained notes are excluded from new changelog entries (`aggregate.rs:588–594`), and the planner emits the commit only for an inference reason (`commands/version.rs:54–64, 269–295`), so the new section loses the commit subject and SHA; a group-triggered release can similarly lose its group note. Keep the planned-severity explanation for the Reason column, but generate changelog entries from the fresh release trigger independently.
</pasted_content id="cd46">
