  - Scenarios:
    - **GitLab subgroup:** `origin` is `https://gitlab.com/group/sub/repo.git` (refused: "remote path must contain exactly an owner and repository").
    - **Bitbucket / Azure DevOps:** their default clone URLs carry a username, e.g. `https://user@bitbucket.org/o/r.git` (refused: "HTTPS userinfo is forbidden").
    - **SSH user:** `ssh://deploy@host/o/r` (refused: "SSH remotes must use the git account").
    - In each case `init` prints "`origin` is not a hosted remote (use https://, ssh:// or git@host:owner/repo)" and `--workflow` gives E299 with no reason. The user is already using `https://` or `ssh://`, so the advice gives them nothing to act on.
  - The accepted forms are worded differently in different places:
  
  | Wording | Where |
  | --- | --- |
  | `ssh://git@` | E132 help, changeset |
  | bare `ssh://` | `OriginRefusal` display (`error.rs:1022`), init note |
  | "hosted HTTPS or SSH URL" | `README.md:161`, `docs/releasing.md:13` |
  
  - Smallest fix:
    - Carry the reason: `InitFacts.origin_not_hosted: Option<&'static str>` instead of `bool`, and put it in the note and the E299 message. The reasons are fixed strings, so nothing the user supplied leaks.
    - Use one constant for the accepted forms ("an https:// or ssh://git@ URL, or git@host:owner/repo, without credentials") in the `OriginRefusal` display and the init note, and copy that wording into the README and `releasing.md`.
  
  **F5's message half and the changeset's "including when there is nothing to release yet" claim are covered under Class 3 (F6).**
  
  **Changeset wording [low]:** line 9 says "`init` prints each note and piece of advice once on stderr". In text mode, "Next steps" and `NO_COMMITS` advice go to stdout (`init.rs:357`, `:284`). Fix: "each note once on stderr".
  
  **Spec drift [low]:** `docs/specs/cli.json` CLI-INIT-01 still says `Detected:` prints "the origin remote URL". It now prints `not hosted` for a non-hosted `origin` (`init.rs:469-474`). Add "or `not hosted`, never the value".
  
  ## Class 2: Mode and order
  
  | Command / mode | stdout | stderr | When the notes appear |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | init, TTY text, prompting | facts, toml, preview, summary; after confirm: next steps, "Initialized…" | all notes once | before the write question; merge note after the write |
  | init, `--yes` text | same as above | Diagnostics once (`finish`) | at the end |
  | init, `--yes` JSON | one report | human output + Diagnostics once | at the end |
  | init, non-TTY without `--yes` | — | E240 after facts (E299 first if a flag needs `origin`) | — |
  | init `--dry-run` | — | rendered once | — |
  | release `--dry-run` text/JSON | preview | unbound note, warnings, pending note: each once, after the preview | — |
  
  **F5 [low, CONFIRMED]: JSON plus prompting shows nothing before the write question and loses everything on decline**
  - `crates/callisto-cli/src/commands/init.rs:302-311`: the `if !json` guard.
  - Scenario: `callisto --format json init` with a terminal on stdin and no `--yes`. On this PR's base, `tty::is_interactive()` checks only stdin, so it prompts.
    - Before `Write callisto.toml?` no notes are shown, including the missing-origin note.
    - Declining prints "Initialization cancelled" to stderr, exits 0, writes no JSON report, and every note is lost.
    - This breaks the contract's "exactly one report on stdout" and is the JSON sibling of open finding #2.
  - It goes away once #638 makes JSON never prompt; the guard then becomes dead code.
  - Fix: if #634 merges first, render before the question in both formats and skip them in `finish`. Otherwise land #638 first and drop the guard.
  
  **Note, not a defect in this PR:** the decline text "Initialization cancelled; nothing was written." differs from the contract's "Cancelled, nothing written." That belongs to #638.
  
  **`release --dry-run --format json`:** the pending hint (and the unbound note) appear only on stderr. `ReleaseDryRunReport` and `ReleaseNothingReport` never carry `pending-version-work`, but the `init` JSON report does.
  - This matches REL-CMD-05 as written. Whether the release report should also include it is your call; adding it is a new permanent JSON key.
  
  ## Class 3: Same set
  
  **F6 [medium, CONFIRMED]: `release --dry-run --package` on a released package with a pending changeset fails with E169 and no hint**
  - `crates/callisto-graph/src/commands/release_decision.rs:285-290` (roster selection), `crates/callisto-cli/src/commands/release.rs:117-121` (`planned?` returns before `pending_version_work`), `error.rs:966`.
  - Scenario: `cargo/a` was released at 1.0.0 and the tag exists. The user adds a changeset for `cargo/a` and runs `callisto release --dry-run --package cargo/a`.
    - `derive_release_roster` finds `cargo/a` tagged and fails with E169: "its on-disk version already matches its last release; there is nothing pending to release".
    - There is pending work, and the hint never prints.
    - The changeset (line 12, plus the `--package` sub-bullet) and REL-CMD-05 both promise the hint "including when there is nothing to release".
  - Smallest fix: in the dry-run branch, when `planned` is `Err(ReleaseSelectionInvalid { reason: NotARelease, .. })`, compute `pending_version_work(&workspace, &selections)` first. If the selection has pending work, print the note before returning the error. Or reword `NotARelease` to say "run `callisto version` first if it has changesets".
  
  **Swept and consistent everywhere else:**
  - **Push URL:** every reader uses the one cached `Workspace::origin()` push URL (`derive.rs:308-309`, `local.rs:90/174/276/313/432/459`, `version.rs:169`, `pending.rs:57`). The deliberate re-read is `recheck_git_remote`.
  - **Init vs preview:** init's `facts.origin` and the preview's `tags_unbound` agree, because both come from `--push` through the same parser.
  - **Fetch URL:** only init's default-branch lookup reads it (`ls-remote --symref origin`, `refs/remotes/origin/HEAD`). That is intended, since it is a branch name, not a binding.
  - **Selection and groups:** pending and the plan narrow through the same `expand_selection`. Private packages are filtered before expansion, and inferred commits use the same `CommitInference` as `version`.
  
  ## Class 4: Secrets
  
  **F7 [low, CONFIRMED]: E120 still shows a token carried in the URL path**
  - `crates/callisto-graph/src/error.rs:980-993` (`ShownRegistry::new`), called at `binding.rs:90`.
  - By the time E120 fires, `raw` has already passed `canonical_registry_binding` (https, no userinfo, query or fragment). So `ShownRegistry` effectively always shows it, and its own checks never trigger on this path.
  - Scenario: Gemfury's npm form `https://npm.fury.io/<TOKEN>/<user>/` in `publishConfig.registry` that is not in `[registries]`. E120's message and Debug output print the token.
  - Fix: show only the registry's scheme and host (`url.origin().ascii_serialization()`), or never show it.
  
  **Swept:**
  - E132 and E299 never hold the remote: `NotHosted` keeps a fixed reason, and the `Origin` Debug output shows only the canonical endpoint.
  - `Detected: origin:` shows the canonical endpoint only when it is hosted and credential-free.
  - `run_quiet` neither echoes nor logs.
  - `Workspace` has no Debug.
  
  **Note:** `Workspace.origin_url` holds the raw push URL, possibly with credentials, in a public field. It does not reach any output today. Caching the parsed `Origin` (which is crate-private) would require a crate-private field instead.
  
  ## Class 5: Input edges
  
  Every refused form below is sent through the one parser and shows only a fixed reason.
  
  | Origin form | Result |
  | --- | --- |
  | missing, local path, `remotes/x.git`, `file://`, `hg::…` | Missing or NotHosted |
  | scp without a user (`host:o/r`) | NotHosted, "URL must have an authority" |
  | scp with another user, `ssh://user@` | NotHosted |
  | `git@host:2222/o/r` | NotHosted (correct: git reads 2222 as a path) |
  | `ssh://git@host:2222/o/r` | accepted |
  | uppercase scheme or host | normalised |
  | trailing `/` or `.git/` | accepted |
  | GitHub Enterprise or GitLab owner/repo | hosted, no GitHub repository |
  | GitLab subgroups, Azure `_git`, userinfo HTTPS | NotHosted, but see F4: the reason is dropped in `init` and E299 |
  | empty `remote.origin.url` | NotHosted |
  | pushInsteadOf / insteadOf rewrites | applied by `get-url --push`, so the hint shows what Callisto parsed |
  
  Registry URL forms: see F7. Nothing further in this class.
  
  ## Class 6: Rust and process count
  
  **F8 [medium, CONFIRMED by trace; not measured]: `release --dry-run` now does a full version-planning pass, adding a git process per auto-release package**
  - `crates/callisto-cli/src/commands/release.rs:119` calls `release_decision.rs:161-179`, which runs `plan_version` with `CommitInference`. That reaches `aggregate.rs:433-468`.
    - With any `release-trigger = "auto"` package: one `git rev-parse --is-shallow-repository`.
    - Then one `git log` per auto package (`access.rs:641`).
  - `release --dry-run` never ran `plan_version` before this PR, so a 50-package auto workspace gains about 51 git processes per dry run. AGENTS.md says that fails review.
  - With the default changeset trigger it adds no git processes. `just bench` likely misses it unless its shapes use `auto`.
  - Smallest fix: compute the hint without inference (`NoInference`, i.e. changesets only) and narrow REL-CMD-05's "or releases inferred from commits"; or keep it and measure it with `CALLISTO_BENCH_SHAPES=1` on an auto shape.
  - Related: in a shallow CI clone with auto packages, `plan_version` fails with ShallowHistory and `unwrap_or_default()` drops the hint silently. That is documented as deliberate, so I did not count it as a separate finding.
  
  **F9 [info]:** `init` reads `origin` twice: `detect_facts` (`graph init.rs:284`) and then the preview's new `Workspace`. That is fewer reads than main and returns the same answer. Pre-seeding `origin_url` for the preview would make it one.
  
  **Public API changes in callisto-graph (all in the 0.x fixed-group minor bump):**
  - `Workspace` gains a public field `origin_url: OnceCell<Option<String>>`, which breaks struct literals.
  - `GraphError::UntrustedNpmRegistry { url: String }` becomes `{ registry: ShownRegistry }`.
  - `GraphError::UnsafeGitRemote { reason }` becomes `{ refusal: OriginRefusal }`.
  - New public `error::ShownRegistry` (no public constructor) and `error::OriginRefusal` (exhaustive, not `#[non_exhaustive]`).
  - `InitFacts` gains a public `origin_not_hosted: bool` (also breaking for struct literals).
  - `commands::init::preview` now returns `(Option<LocalReleasePlan>, Vec<ReleasePackageId>)`.
  - New `commands::pending_version_work`.
  
  callisto-model gains `DiagnosticCode::PendingVersionWork`. The enum is `#[non_exhaustive]`, and the wire name `pending-version-work` is permanent.
  
  **Swept, nothing further:**
  - No `_` arms on our enums in the new code (`Origin`, `GitRemoteRequirement`, `OriginRefusal`, and the preview `match` are all exhaustive).
  - No new `unwrap` or `expect` outside tests.
  - The other swallowed errors (`forge_repository_for(...).ok()` in `version.rs` and `pending.rs`) behave as they did on main.
  
  ## Per-class status
  1. Messages vs behaviour: F1, F2, F3, F4, the changeset line 9 wording, CLI-INIT-01. Nothing further.
  2. Mode and order: F5; the JSON pending hint is your call. Nothing further.
  3. Same set: F6. Nothing further.
  4. Secrets: F7. Nothing further.
  5. Input edges: only F4. Nothing further.
  6. Rust and process count: F8, plus F9 as info; API changes listed. Nothing further.

  ## Class 1: terminal rendering environment
  
  **1. [Medium] CONFIRMED: frame height overflows in more places than PAGE=15, and wide labels make it worse.**
  - **Mechanism:** inquire's `FrameState` (ui/frame_renderer.rs:86-95) wraps rows to the terminal width itself, so width alone is handled. Nothing anywhere uses terminal height. A redraw moves up by the previous frame's row count (`move_cursor_to`, :363-376). Any frame taller than the screen corrupts every later redraw.
  - **Siblings the page-size fix must cover too:**
    - add.rs:277-281 and :293-297, the MAJOR and MINOR multi-selects. They have no `with_page_size`, so inquire's default of 7 applies: 1 prompt row + 7 options + 1 help row = 9 rows.
    - init.rs:73, the `Select` for the versioning question and PRODUCT_PROMPT (one row per binary package). Default page 7, so 9 rows.
  - **Width multiplies rows:** each option or prompt longer than the width becomes 2 or more rows.
    - The first prompt is "? Packages in this change (Space toggles, Enter confirms, Esc cancels)", about 71 columns.
    - inquire's MultiSelect help is about 70 columns.
    - At 60 columns, a label like `npm/@org/some-long-package-name (changed)` plus the "> [x] " prefix wraps, so 15 options take 30+ rows.
    - Scenario: VS Code panel at 60x14 with 8 selected packages. The MAJOR prompt draws 1-2 + 7 + 2 rows, and every arrow key smears the list.
  - **Smallest fix:** one helper in tty.rs used by all five call sites (add.rs:178, :277, :293, init.rs:73 for both selects). Take `crossterm::terminal::size()`, the same source inquire uses (see item 9). Page = clamp((rows − rows for prompt and help at this width − 1) / rows per option, 1..). Truncate labels to the width minus the 6-column prefix so each option stays one row.
  
  **2. [Low] CONFIRMED, contract does not cover it: TERM=dumb still prompts.**
  - `Prompts::allowed` (tty.rs) checks only `is_terminal`, and neither inquire nor crossterm looks at TERM.
  - Scenario: Emacs `M-x shell` (a pty with TERM=dumb) running `callisto add`. Cursor-movement and bracketed-paste escapes print as junk, and redraws pile up.
  - Git skips VISUAL when TERM=dumb.
  - Fix, if the owner wants it: treat `TERM=dumb` as "cannot prompt" in `Prompts::allowed`, giving E214/E240. That needs a contract line.
  
  **3. [Low] CONFIRMED: non-UTF-8 locale gets mojibake help text and wrong wrap math.**
  - inquire's default help strings use "↑↓ → ←" (prompts/multiselect/mod.rs:219, select/mod.rs:209). On a non-UTF-8 terminal each arrow shows as 3 glyphs, but unicode-width counts 1, so the help row can wrap one row more than inquire assumes.
  - Fix: pass an ASCII `with_help_message(...)` on the three MultiSelects and init's Select. That can go in the item-1 helper.
  
  **4. Note: mid-prompt resize is not redrawn.**
  - `CrosstermKeyReader::read_key` (terminal/crossterm.rs:70-84) drops `Event::Resize`, so nothing redraws until the next key, and reflow then depends on the emulator.
  - Callisto can't fix this; keeping frames short (item 1) limits the damage. Document it, if anything.
  
  Checked and not an issue: NO_COLOR is honoured (render_config.rs:352), glyphs are ASCII under both configs, CJK width comes from unicode-width, and the review screen and long summaries are plain `eprintln`, never redrawn. After the editor exits, the next prompt starts a fresh renderer with bracketed paste re-enabled.

  ## Class 4: input edges
  
  **12. [Low] CONFIRMED: editor output keeps a BOM and CRLF.**
  - add.rs:210 calls `Summary::new(&text)`, which only trims, and `trim` does not remove U+FEFF.
  - Scenario: an editor that saves with a BOM, or Notepad saving CRLF. The changeset body starts with an invisible BOM or ends lines in `\r`, and that reaches the changelog.
  - Fix: in `Summary::new`, `strip_prefix('\u{FEFF}')` and replace `\r\n` with `\n`.
  
  **13. Note: a typed target list like `aarch64-apple-darwin,` aborts init.**

  - `--summary` that is blank, a newline or very long.

  ## Class 5: ending a run
  
  Every ending matches the contract:
  - Esc or Ctrl-C at every prompt goes through `tty::prompt` to `Cancelled`, then E343 and exit 130.
  - Declining, or an editor that saves nothing or exits non-zero: `Cancelled…`, exit 0.
  - External SIGINT at a prompt: signal 2. During the editor it is ignored; afterwards the default comes back.
  - Nothing is written before the final confirm, in either command.
  
  **14. [Low] Accepted limit: the temp file leaks on SIGHUP or SIGTERM while the editor is open.**
  - tty.rs:72-85: the `NamedTempFile` is removed on every Rust return path, but not on death by signal (for example closing the terminal window while vim is open). That leaves `$TMPDIR/callisto-*.txt`.
  - No fix without signal handling, which the contract forbids. Document it, or accept it.
  
  **15. Note: ticking nothing ends the run as an error.**
  - Enter with nothing ticked gives E273, exit 1 (add.rs:271). The contract's list of endings doesn't include it.
  - If it should re-ask instead: add a `with_validator` requiring at least one pick on the first MultiSelect.
  
  ## Class 6: Rust and tests
  
  **16. [Low] CONFIRMED: a guarded wildcard arm on our own enum.**
  - main.rs:37 uses `_ if matches!(err, CliError::Cancelled)` inside `match err.exit_kind()`. error.rs:90 adds `Self::Cancelled => ExitKind::Runtime` right before an existing `_` arm, which says nothing about the real exit code (130).
  - Fix: `let exit = if matches!(err, CliError::Cancelled) { 130 } else { match err.exit_kind() { Usage => 2, Runtime => 1 } }` and drop error.rs:90. Or add an `ExitKind` variant.
  
  **17. [Low] CONFIRMED: two PTY tests are timing-dependent and can fail at the 20 s deadline.**
  - add_interactive_pty_tests.rs:302-303 and :467-468 wait for the 2nd "Changelog summary". That count is reached by the first prompt's answered-row redraw, before raw mode is turned off and before the editor runs.
  - The keys are then typed after a fixed 200 ms sleep. If the editor (`sh` in the not-UTF-8 case) or a loaded machine takes longer, they arrive in cooked mode. ICRNL turns `\r` into `\n`, and in raw mode crossterm treats `\n` as Ctrl+J, not Enter (event/sys/unix/parse.rs:99). So "Fix b" is never submitted.
  - Fix: wait for text only the new prompt draws, such as `"summary (Esc cancels)"`, which is drawn after raw mode is on.
  
  **18. [Low] CONFIRMED: PTY tests don't control the terminal size inquire sees.**
  - crossterm's `window_size()` opens `/dev/tty` first (terminal/sys/unix.rs:70/87). `Session::launch` (tests :76-111) never makes the pty the child's controlling terminal (no setsid or TIOCSCTTY).
  - Locally, inquire lays out to the developer's terminal, not the 120x30 set by `tcsetwinsize`. In CI there is no controlling tty, so it falls back to stdout, the pty.
  - This matters for the page-size work: a small-terminal PTY test would only reproduce in CI.
  - Fix: spawn through a crate that sets the controlling tty safely (for example a `portable-pty` dev-dependency); `pre_exec` is unsafe and `unsafe_code` is forbidden. Or skip size-dependent assertions when `/dev/tty` opens in the test process.
  
  **19. [Low] init can prompt without the type-level proof add uses.**
  - add takes a `tty::Prompts` proof (add.rs:163-165). init reduces it to `bool` with `.is_ok()` (init.rs:93), and `TerminalPrompter` needs no proof.
  - Fix: pass `Option<tty::Prompts>` into `init::run`, or hold the proof in `TerminalPrompter`.

  **Leftover custom terminal handling: none in shipped code.** There is no termios, escape parsing or timing code in `crates/callisto-cli/src`. `dialoguer` is gone from `Cargo.lock`. `rustix` with `pty` and `termios` is now only a unix dev-dependency. The only signal code left is the SIGINT guard while `$EDITOR` runs (`tty.rs:208-239`), which matches the contract and git's `launch_editor`.
  
  ### 1. [Low] The first package list tells you "Enter picks none", then fails with E273
  - **Status:** CONFIRMED.
  - **Where:** `crates/callisto-cli/src/tty.rs:39-44` and `crates/callisto-cli/src/commands/add.rs:255-257`.
  - **Scenario:** every package is released and unchanged, or detecting changes failed, so nothing is marked. The hint reads "Enter picks none, ...". The validator accepts the empty line, `ask` gets `[]`, and the run ends with `AddNoPackagesSelected` (E273, exit 1). Following the on-screen hint ends the run in an error.
  - **Smallest fix:** give `pick` an at-least-one flag for the first list so the validator asks again ("pick at least one"). That matches the pending contract addition "Ticking no packages in add re-asks instead of E273", and the E273 path becomes unreachable. If the owner has not approved that yet, the minimum is a different hint on the first list: "type numbers, ranges (1-3) or names; Esc cancels".
  
  ### 2. [Low] Two numeric package names can only be picked one at a time through an undocumented range
  - **Status:** CONFIRMED.
  - **Where:** `tty.rs:110-115`.
  - **Scenario:** npm packages `2` (changed) and `1` (unchanged). Changed packages sort first, so the list is row 1 = `2`, row 2 = `1`.
    - Typing `1` matches package `1` by name (row 2) and also row 1 by number, so it is refused as "both a listed name and another entry's number". Typing `2` is refused the same way.
    - Only the degenerate ranges `1-1` and `2-2` pick one package; the reason text never says so.
    - Display names are bare unless two ecosystems share a name (`identity.rs:130-137`, and your PTY test writes `42: patch`), so this happens in single-ecosystem npm or PyPI workspaces. It cannot happen in `init`, because Cargo names cannot start with a digit.
  - **Smallest fix:** say the way out in the reason, e.g. "``1`` is row 2's name and row 1's number; type `2-2` for the package or `1-1` for row 1". An alternative is to also accept the qualified `ecosystem/name` id as a listed name.
  
  ### 3. [Low] The review prints package names raw, while the list escapes them
  - **Status:** CONFIRMED.
  - **Where:** `add.rs:320`, sibling of `tty.rs:63-64`.
  - **Scenario:** an npm `package.json` name with a control character, e.g. `"a\u001b[2Jb"`. Nothing rejects it: `validate_prefixed_remainder` checks only empty, leading `-` and `..`. `print_list` escapes it, which its comment justifies ("must not drive the terminal"), but `review()` then writes `entry.name` raw to stderr.
  - **Smallest fix:** use `entry.name.escape_debug()` in `review()`.
  
  ### 4. [Low] PTY tests type into list prompts before inquire has switched the terminal to raw mode
  - **Status:** CONFIRMED as a race; I did not observe it fail.
  - **Where:** `crates/callisto-cli/tests/add_interactive_pty_tests.rs`. `after()` is at :158-167; the affected waits are on the "Packages in this change" heading and on "MAJOR" and "MINOR" (:236-238, :254-256, :267, :300, :316-317, :326-328, and others).
  - **Cause:** those headings are printed by `print_list` before `Text::prompt()` calls `enable_raw_mode` (inquire `terminal/crossterm.rs:89-90`). The only guard is a 200 ms sleep.
  - **What goes wrong if a key arrives early:**
    - `\r` becomes `\n` through ICRNL, and crossterm reads `\n` in raw mode as Ctrl-J, not Enter (crossterm `parse.rs:95-101`). The test then hangs until its 20 s deadline.
    - `\x03` with ISIG on goes to a pty that has no controlling process, so it is lost.
    - This is most likely under coverage-instrumented `just ci`.
  - **Smallest fix:** for list steps, wait on the prompt's own text ("Your answer", counted per list) instead of the heading. inquire draws that text only after it enters raw mode. The summary step already waits on inquire's own prompt text.
  
  ### 5. [Low] Tests and changeset drift
  - **Product-package test doesn't check what it names.** `init_asks_for_exactly_one_product_package_by_number_or_name` (:744-760):
    - Its second `after("pick exactly one", 1, ...)` is already satisfied by the first refusal, so it never waits for, or proves, that `1 2` was refused. It should wait for the count to reach 2.
    - It picks only by name, never by number. Fix: rename it, or add a number pick.
  - **Stale scaffolding from the removed reader.** At :285 the comment ("A quick double Esc is two Escape keys, not an arrow's sequence; `stty -iexten` leaves Ctrl-C a key"), `start_without_iexten` and the `termios` feature now only exercise inquire's key handling, which is dismissed by policy. The comment is wrong for the current design. Fix: drop the iexten cases, the helper and the `termios` feature, or reword the comment.
  - **Changeset gap.** `.changeset/sharp-badgers-hunt.md` says only `init` stops prompting with stderr redirected. `add` changed too: the old `tty::is_interactive()` checked stdin only, so `callisto add 2>log` and `callisto --format json add` used to open the wizard and now exit 2 with E214. Fix: a bullet saying `add` and `init` prompt only with stdin and stderr on a terminal. Whether this needs a `**Breaking:**` paragraph under the AGENTS.md rules is the owner's call.

Declining the review in `add` (stderr) or `init` prints "Cancelled, nothing written." and exits 0. An editor that saves nothing, saves only whitespace, or exits non-zero exits 0. An unusable editor warns once, then asks inline and keeps the answers. Whitespace at the inline prompt re-asks. A blank `--summary` fails before any prompt. External SIGINT takes the default action, as documented.

     - The iexten cases, `start_without_iexten`, the `launch` parameter and the stale double-Esc comment are all gone (test file lines 307-322).
     - The problem is the feature removal; see issue A.
  5. **ok.**
     - `init.after("pick exactly one", 2, …)` is at test file line 832, and `init_picks_the_product_package_by_number` at lines 837-841.
     - The changeset (`.changeset/sharp-badgers-hunt.md:17-19`) matches the code: `Prompts::detect` is at `tty.rs:17-19`. E214 is `NotATty`, which `error.rs:63` maps to the usage kind (exit 2), and its help names `--package` and `--summary`. This agrees with the contract's "Exit codes … 2 usage".
  
  **New issues**
  
  - **A (Medium, CONFIRMED): the `termios` dev-feature is still needed.**
    - `crates/callisto-cli/Cargo.toml:61` now has only `features = ["pty"]`.
    - But `add_interactive_pty_tests.rs:106-115` still calls `rustix::termios::tcsetwinsize` and `rustix::termios::Winsize`, and rustix only has that module behind `#[cfg(feature = "termios")]` (rustix-1.1.6 `src/lib.rs:298`).
    - It compiles today only because crossterm 0.29.0, pulled in by `inquire` with features `["crossterm"]`, enables rustix's `termios` on unix.
    - Scenario: if inquire changes backend or crossterm drops that feature, the pty test crate stops compiling.
    - Fix: put `"termios"` back. Only the `tcgetattr`/`tcsetattr` use went away.
  - **B (Low, PLAUSIBLE): the collision reason can suggest the word you just typed.**
    - When a package has no canonical manifest, `canonical_id()` returns its bare id (`callisto-model/src/package.rs:33-43`). `Listed.id` is then the bare name, and `tty.rs:147-149` says "type `1` for the package", which is the colliding word itself.
    - The unit test at `tty.rs:326` (`names(&["2","b"])`) already produces exactly that message. I do not think the wizard can list such a package, so this is low impact.
    - Similarly, the `N-N` row suggestion collides again if another package is literally named `1-1`. This is contrived.
  - **C (Low): duplicate matches go silently to the first row.** `position()` at `tty.rs:138` returns the first package whose name or id equals the word. If one package's bare name equals another's id (in practice only an odd Go module path such as `npm/foo`), the word picks the first package with no ambiguity reason. This behaviour existed for names before this batch; the batch widens it to ids.
  
  **No regressions found in**
  - Answer parsing: numbers, backwards or out-of-list ranges, out-of-list numbers falling back to a name match, duplicates (deduplicated by the BTreeSet), empty Enter (marked rows, or none), `pick_one` exactness. `pick_one` uses `Listed::by_id` where name equals id, so it behaves as before.
  - Major/minor mapping: majors are tracked by index and minors by name (`add.rs:280-315`), as before.
  - Code safety: no `unwrap()` added outside tests; the new `expect`s state their invariant.
  - Test hangs: every wait in the new tests has a 20 s deadline assert.
  - Test coverage: every renamed or new test still checks what its name says.

  I reviewed 60c645cef by reading the code only, without building or running anything. All three fixes are correct and tested. I found one real regression on Windows and three smaller problems.
  
  **Fix 1 (an answer of only separators): ok.**
  - `crates/callisto-cli/src/tty.rs:116-123`: a line that is blank after trimming keeps the marked set, or gives "pick exactly one" at a pick-one question. Any other line goes to `choices`.
  - `tty.rs:172-174`: when no word is left after dropping the separators, `choices` returns "type at least one number, range or name".
  - Tested by `an_answer_of_only_separators_is_asked_again` (calls `choices` directly) and the existing `an_empty_line_keeps_the_marked_ones_and_one_pick_needs_exactly_one` (`tty.rs:469-481`, covers `answer`).
  - Small side effect: `,,` at a pick-one question now says "type at least one ..." rather than "pick exactly one". That reads fine.
  
  **Fix 2 (`#N` always means rows): ok, and no suggestion can collide.**
  - In `add`, `id` is `pkg.canonical_id().display_name()` (`commands/add.rs:375-378`). For prefixed packages it always contains `/`, so it never parses as a number and never starts with `#`.
  - The only case where `id == word` is a bare id with no canonical manifest. That case now suggests `#index`, which is always rows (`tty.rs:156-160`).
  - Range collisions suggest `#a-b` (`tty.rs:151-154`). That is right.
  - A name starting with `#`: npm, cargo, PEP 508 and Go module paths cannot contain `#`. Only a bare or hand-written name could. Such a package can't be typed by name, but its row `#N` always works, so it can still be picked.
  - Edge inputs:
    - `#` alone and `#x` give "`#` is not a row number or range".
    - `##1` and `#1-` fail the same way.
    - `#1 b` mixes rows and names correctly.
    - `#9` is rejected.
  - Tests: `a_hash_always_means_rows_so_every_suggestion_is_unambiguous`, the updated `the_reason_for_an_ambiguous_word_says_how_to_pick_each`, and the pty test at `add_interactive_pty_tests.rs:744`.
  
  **Fix 3 (Windows `$EDITOR` paths): ok for what it claims.**
  - `tty.rs:263-291`: whitespace inside double quotes is kept and every backslash is kept.
  - A trailing backslash is kept. `"C:\dir\"` gives `C:\dir\` rather than following the Windows convention where `\"` is an escaped quote. That is more useful for paths, though it differs from that convention.
  - An empty `""` becomes an empty word. If it is the program, the error is "`` did not start", which is acceptable.
  - An unclosed quote gives "it does not split into words: a double quote is not closed", matching the unix prefix.
  - Unix is unchanged: the same `shell_words::split` call with the same error text (`tty.rs:264-266`).
  - Tested with `windows=true` on every platform by `windows_editor_settings_keep_backslashes`.
  
  **New issues:**
  
  1. **[Low-Medium] Single-quoted Windows editor settings no longer work.** (`tty.rs:269-283`)
     - Git for Windows runs `$EDITOR` through sh, and its docs show single-quoted paths, e.g. `EDITOR="'C:/Program Files/Notepad++/notepad++.exe' -multiInst -nosession"`.
     - Before this commit, `shell_words` handled that. Now the `'` is kept as a literal character and the value splits at the space, so the program becomes `'C:/Program`.
     - The result is a "did not start" warning and the summary asked inline. It fails gracefully, but it is a regression for setups that work with git.
     - Suggested fix: also treat `'...'` as quotes in the Windows splitter, and add a test for it.
  
  2. **[Low] Errors for `#0` and `#2-1` drop the `#`.** (`tty.rs:141-147`, `tty.rs:186-190`)
     - `rows` loses its `#` before it reaches `numbered`, so the user sees "`0` is not in the list (1-3)" and "`2-1` runs backwards".
     - The messages are correct, but they quote a different word from the one typed. Passing the full word through for the message would fix it.
  
  3. **[Low] The spec and the prompt hints don't fully describe `#`.**
     - `docs/specs/cli.json` CLI-ADD-03 says the reason names "the `ecosystem/name` id, or `#N` for a row". The code also suggests `#N` for the package when there is no separate id, and `#a-b` for a range.
     - The hint lines at `tty.rs:62-67` and `tty.rs:76` never mention `#`, so users only find it through an error message.
  
  Nothing I found writes wrong data or picks the wrong package silently. Every ambiguous word is asked again.

  ## Remaining issues
  
  **1. [Low] Report fields claiming something not done: the "could not check" note reuses the "pending work found" code**
  - Location: `crates/callisto-cli/src/commands/release.rs:83-96` sets `DiagnosticCode::PendingVersionWork`. `crates/callisto-cli/src/commands/init.rs:338` puts it into the init JSON report. The variant is documented at `crates/callisto-model/src/diagnostic.rs:176-178` as "found version work ... pending".
  - Scenario: `callisto init --yes --format json`, where the pending check fails (for example an ambiguous changeset entry). `diagnostics[]` then carries `pending-version-work` (warning, "could not check..."). A consumer keyed on the code reads that as pending work found.
  - The two commands also disagree: `release --dry-run --format json` leaves the unchecked note out of JSON (`release.rs:193`, only `pending.diagnostic()`), which matches REL-CMD-05.
  - Smallest fix, no new permanent code: widen the variant doc to "pending version work found, or the check for it could not run" (and the matching errors/spec text). The alternative is dropping `unchecked_diagnostic()` from init's JSON at `init.rs:338` to match release.
  
  **2. [Low] Messages vs behaviour: the after-write advice comes out of order, and partly on stdout**
  - Location: `crates/callisto-cli/src/commands/init.rs:408-424`, called at `:379` and `:458`.
  - In text mode, `announce_workflow` writes "Next steps" to stdout straight away. The "Merging to `main` publishes: require pull requests..." warning goes onto the queue and only reaches stderr at `:384` (or `:467` for the workflow-only path), so it prints after the next steps.
  - CLI-WF-01 says init "prints that merging publishes, advises..., and lists the next steps", in that order. The changeset (`.changeset/tough-pandas-leap.md:9`) says "each note and piece of advice once on stderr", but in text mode the next steps go to stdout.
  - Smallest fix: emit the queued notes right after pushing the merge note and before writing the next steps. That means splitting `announce_workflow` so it returns the diagnostic and the next steps write is separate, or calling `notes.emit(err)` before that write. Separately, reword the changeset bullet to say "each note once on stderr".
